We remark that the cartesian-ness of the left-hand-side is by Definition
8.1.2
.

It thus suffices to prove that the map on the bottom left, bottom right and top right vertices are equivalences. This follows from Theorem
1.2.2
, Corollary
8.1.5
and Corollary
7.4.1
respectively.

∎

8.3.
A counterexample

Let
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an}}_{\mathbb Q}
be an animated
\mathbb Q
-algebra and denote by
B\mathbb G_{a,R}:=B\mathbb G_{a}\times\operatorname{Spec}R
. Let
\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]:=\mathbb Q[x]/x^{2}
. By
HH
(resp.
HC^{-}
) below we mean Hochschild homology (resp. negative cyclic homology) relative to
\mathbb Q
. Our goal in this section is to show that AS-completion theorem for
B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]}
(viewed as a stack over
B\mathrm{SL_{2}}
, as in Remark
7.3.2
) does
not
hold. Our
argument will rely on Levy’s generalization of Dundas–Goodwillie–McCarthy theorem for
(-1)
-connective rings

[
Lev22
]
and the identification of
S^{1}
-equivariant structure on
HH(B\mathbb G_{a})
.

1. Note that
B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}
is an affine stack in the sense of Toën, corepresented by the algebra

\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q)
(see
[
Toë06
, Définition 2.2.4 and Lemma 2.2.5]
).
In particular,
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
is generated under colimits and shifts by the
structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}
, and
\End_{\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})}\simeq\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q)

(see
[
Lur11
, Proposition 4.5.2]
). Since by
[
HR15
, Theorem A(1)]
perfect complexes over
B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}

compactly generate
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
, this induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q)),

and a further
S^{1}
-equivariant identification

HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq HH(\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q))\simeq(\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q))^{\otimes S^{1}}\simeq\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}]),

where
\mathbb Q[S^{1}]
is the regular
S^{1}
-representation (given by singular homology
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Q)
). Note that
H^{*}(\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}]))\simeq\mathbb Q[x]\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[x]
and that
\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}])
is an
S^{1}
-equivariant module over
\mathbb Q[x]
(with the trivial
S^{1}
-action on the latter).

Let
\mathrm{pt}\rightarrow B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}\rightarrow\mathrm{pt}
be the natural maps; they split
HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
as

HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq\mathbb Q\oplus\overline{HH}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}).

We have a natural
S^{1}
-equivariant map
\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\rightarrow\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}])\simeq HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
; it’s easy to see (looking at what this map does on cohomology) that the map

\mathbb Q[x]\otimes\Omega\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\rightarrow HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})

extended via
\mathbb Q[x]
-module structure is an isomorphism onto
\overline{HH}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
. To summarize, we get an
S^{1}
-equivariant identification

HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq\mathbb Q\oplus\left(\Omega\mathbb Q[x]\otimes\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\right),

where the actions on
\mathbb Q
and
\mathbb Q[x]
are trivial. Taking
S^{1}
-fixed points then yields an identification

HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq HC^{-}(\mathbb Q)\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[x]

where
HC^{-}(\mathbb Q)\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(BS^{1},\mathbb Q)
. Here recall that
S^{1}
-fixed points of
V\otimes\mathbb Q[S^{1}]
are given by
V
for any
S^{1}
-module
V
.

2. By
[
BZFN10
, Theorem 1.2(1)]
we have
\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a,R})\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\otimes\mathrm{Perf}(R)
; this gives (again,
S^{1}
-equivariant) splitting as

HH(B\mathbb G_{a,R})\simeq HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\otimes HH(R)\simeq HH(R)\oplus\left(\Omega HH(R)[x]\otimes\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\right).

Taking
S^{1}
-fixed points gives

HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,R})=HC^{-}(R)\oplus\Omega HH(R)[x].

3. Recall from Remark
7.3.2
that we have
R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq\mathbb Q[[x]]\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[[x]]
. Moreover, the natural map
HH(\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\rightarrow R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
can be identified
with the map

\mathbb Q[x]\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[x]\rightarrow\mathbb Q[[x]]\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[[x]]

sending
x
to
x
. The
S^{1}
-module
R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
is a module over its 0-th cohomology
\mathbb Q[[x]]
(on which the action is trivial) and, arguing as for
HH(\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
, we get an
S^{1}
-equivariant decomposition

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq\mathbb Q\oplus\left(\Omega\mathbb Q[[x]]\otimes\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\right).

Passing to
S^{1}
-fixed points (and commuting them through the right Kan extension) we get

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})\simeq HC^{-}(\mathbb Q)\oplus\Omega\mathbb Q[[x]].

4. Analogously, for
R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(B\mathbb G_{a,R})
we get an
S^{1}
-equivariant splitting as

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(B\mathbb G_{a,R})\simeq HH(R)\oplus\left(\Omega HH(R)[[x]]\otimes\mathbb Q[S^{1}]\right);

indeed, left hand side is given by functions on the formal loop stack of
B\mathbb G_{a,R}
. Arguing as in Example
7.2.11
(2)
, the classical locus of
\mathcal{L}B\mathbb G_{a,R}
is identified with
\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}\times B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}\times\operatorname{Spec}\pi_{0}(R)
, with constant loops given by
\{e\}\times B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}\times\operatorname{Spec}\pi_{0}(R)
. This allows to identify
R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HH(B\mathbb G_{a,R})
with the
x
-adic completion of
HH(R)
, giving the formula above.
Applying
S^{1}
-fixed points gives a formula for negative cyclic homology

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,R})\simeq HC^{-}(R)\oplus\Omega HH(R)[[x]].

5. Following Remark
7.3.2
we view
B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}
as the quotient (of the general affine space
SL_{2}/\mathbb G_{a}
) by
SL_{2}
. We also have a map
B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]}\rightarrow B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}\rightarrow BSL_{2}
. As discussed in loc.cit. the image of the AS-ideal
I_{SL_{2}}\subset K_{0}(BSL_{2})
in
HH_{0}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})
(and, consequently,
HH_{0}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]})
) is zero.

6. To pass to K-theory, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]})^{\wedge}_{I_{SL_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]})^{\wedge}_{I_{SL_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q[\varepsilon]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})^{\wedge}_{I_{SL_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q})^{\wedge}_{I_{SL_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dAff}}HC^{-}(B\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}).}

It follows from
[
Lev22
, Theorem B]
that the functor

\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{Fib}\left(K(\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a})\otimes\mathcal{C})\to HC^{-}(\mathrm{Perf}(B\mathbb G_{a})\otimes\mathcal{C})\right)

is a truncating localizing invariant. Hence by
[
LT19
, Corollary 3.6]
it is also nilinvariant.
Hence, the left square in the diagram is a pullback square.
If the AS completion theorem were true in this case, then the outer rectangle would be a pullback as well, by
Proposition
4.4.3
applied to
\operatorname{Fib}(K\to HC^{-})
.
By the pasting law for pullbacks, this means that the right square would be a pullback.
However, it follows from the computations above that that commutative square may be identified with the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(\mathbb Q[\varepsilon])\oplus\Omega HH(\mathbb Q[\varepsilon])[x]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(\mathbb Q[\varepsilon])\oplus\Omega HH(\mathbb Q[\varepsilon])[[x]]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(\mathbb Q)\oplus\Omega HH(\mathbb Q)[x]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(\mathbb Q)\oplus\Omega HH(\mathbb Q)[[x]]}

which is not pullback. This follows, for example, by noting that the homotopy groups of the left vertical fibre is countable while the ones on the right are the uncountable.

9.
Applications

In this application section, we offer two consequences of our main theorem. One application reads the theorem from right to left and the other from left to right.

9.1.
Functorial pushforwards

For this application, we fix a field
k
of charateristic zero, although some of the preliminary discussion works more generally. We begin by recalling some functoriality of the formation of perfect complexes. We first work in the generality of perfect derived stacks; given a morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
we have the pullback functor
f^{*}:\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})
. This functor preserves perfect complexes and thus defines a functor
f^{*}:\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{Y})
. If
f
is furthermore representbale, proper, laft and is of finite tor-amplitude then the right adjoint preserves perfect complexes producing the functor

f_{*}:\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{Y}).

We might as well define a morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of perfect derived stacks to be
quasiperfect

f_{*}
preserves perfect complexes; this class of morphisms evidently includes those which are representable, proper, laft and is of finite tor-amplitude. Fixing a base prestack
\mathcal{X}
, we contemplate the following
\infty
-category of spans

\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{qcqs,perf}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect}),

whose objects are representable morphisms
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
where the domain is a perfect derived stack and morphisms are spans

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
f
is any representable morphism and
g
is a quasiperfect and representable. For a precise construction of this
\infty
-category see
[
Bar17
]
.

Construction 9.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be perfect stack. As explained by Barwick in
[
Bar17
, Example D]
, using the base change explained in
[
Lur18
, Proposition 6.3.4.1]
the formation of perfect complexes assembles into a functor

\mathrm{Perf}:\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{qcqs,perf}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\infty}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{X})).

Postcomposing with the functor of algebraic
K
-theory we get

K\circ\mathrm{Perf}:\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{qcqs,perf}}_{\mathcal{X}},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\infty}(\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{X})),

with a coherent pushforward functoriality.

Now, we work with
\mathcal{X}=BG
, where
G
is a reductive group in characteristic zero.

Lemma 9.1.2
.

There is a natural factorization:

(9.1.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{K(BG)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{qcqs,perf}}_{BG},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spt}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forget}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\circ\mathrm{Perf}}

Proof.

After the factorization explained in (
3.2.4
), the only thing to verify is that for any quasiperfect map
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
the pushforward map
f_{*}:K(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow K(\mathcal{Y})
is a
K(BG)
-module map. This boils down to the projection formula, which is a consequence of the fact that
f
is schematic and quasicompact (since it is a
BG
-morphism between two representable derived algebraic stacks which are assumed to be qc) and thus the projection formula holds by
[
GR17b
, Chapter 3, Lemma 3.2.4]
.
∎

By the functoriality of completion of modules along ideals explained in Section
5.1
Lemma
9.1.2
gives us a functor:

(-)^{\wedge}_{I_{G}}\circ K\circ\mathrm{Perf}:\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{qcqs,perf}}_{BG},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{K(BG)}\xrightarrow{\text{forget}}\mathrm{Spt}.

Now, replacing
\mathrm{dAlgStk}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{BG}
with the smaller, full subcategory,
\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG}
, ANS-stacks equipped with a map to
BG
such that
\mathrm{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow BG
is finite type, our main theorem identifying
R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K
with the Atiyah-Segal completion of
K
-theory shows that

Theorem 9.1.4

(Pushforward structure)
.

The functor

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K:(\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spt},

admits a canonical extension along the faithful functor
\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG}\rightarrow\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})
:

R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K:\mathbf{Corr}(\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{quasiperfect})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spt},

such that, on
\mathrm{dSch}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}\cap\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG}\subset\mathrm{ANS}^{\mathrm{clft}}_{BG}
(the subcategory of those
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow BG
where
\mathcal{X}
is a qcqs derived scheme), the pushforward functoriality agrees with the one induced by pushforward of perfect complexes.

We remark that Theorem
9.1.4
makes no reference to the Atiyah-Segal ideal or the Atiyah-Segal completion. It produces a coherent transfer structure on the right Kan-extended
K
-theory for a large class of derived stacks. As emphasized throughout the text, the right Kan extended theory does not have any
a priori
to extend to a localizing invariant or something close to it. Without identifying it with the completion of a localizing invariant, such a coherent transfer structure would be very difficult to produce.

9.2.
Motivic filtrations

In this section we answer the question of how to endow the
K
-theory of a derived stacks in characteristic zero with a motivic filtration.

9.2.1.
Motivic filtrations on derived algebraic spaces

We refer to
[
Bou24
,
KS24
]
for general accounts of the motivic filtration in mixed characteristic situations and Appendix
C
for a record of its extension to derived algebraic spaces. Since we work over
\mathbb{Q}
, we only need to refer to the motivic filrations of
[
EM23
]
. We define a motivic filtration on derived algebraic stacks over
\mathbb{Q}
-as follows.

Construction 9.2.2
.

On the category of derived algebraic stacks over
\mathbb{Q}
, we define
\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\star}K
as the following pullback in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{FilSpt})
-valued presheaves (where the terms on the right are given the constant filtration):

(9.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\star}K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dAff}}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\star}K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K.}

Since the motivic filtration on algebraic spaces is
\mathbb{N}
-indexed, we have equivalences:

\operatorname*{colim}_{\star\rightarrow-\infty}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\star}K\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Fil}^{0}_{\mathrm{mot}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K;

in other words the filtration is exhaustive. Taking graded pieces gives us the following candidate:

Definition 9.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived algebraic stack over
\mathbb{Q}
. We define its
motivic cohomology in weight
j
as

\mathbb{Z}(j)^{\mathrm{mot}}(\mathcal{X}):=\mathrm{gr}^{j}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(\mathcal{X})[-2j].

In other words, it is given by the right Kan extension of motivic cohomology from derived algebraic spaces.

We can justify that Definition
9.2.4
is a good definition at least for certain ANS stacks, due to our main theorem. More precisely the motivic cohomology of
\mathcal{X}
are the graded pieces of a certain non-complete filtration on the
K
-theory of
\mathcal{X}
whose completion coincides with the Atiyah-Segal completion. To formulate this theorem we recall how completion along a filtration is defined. Let
\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K:=\mathrm{cofib}(\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\geqslant j}K\rightarrow K)
so that we have an inverse system

K\rightarrow\cdots\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K\rightarrow\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j-1}K\rightarrow\cdots\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<1}K\rightarrow 0.

The
motivic completed
K
-theory
of a derived algebraic stack
\mathcal{X}
is set to be

K(\mathcal{X})^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{mot}}:=\operatorname*{lim}_{j}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K(\mathcal{X}).

The next lemma shows that motivic completion of
K
-theory is equivalent to the right Kan extended theory:

Lemma 9.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-derived algebraic stack such that
\mathcal{X}
can be written as a colimit in Nisnevich sheaves:
\mathcal{X}\simeq\operatorname*{colim}^{\mathrm{Nis}}X_{\alpha}
where
X_{\alpha}
is a derived algebraic space for which
\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\star}K(X_{\alpha})
are completed filtrations. Then there is a canonical equivalence

K(\mathcal{X})^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{mot}}\rightarrow R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K(\mathcal{X}).

Proof.

By construction, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\mathcal{X})^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{mot}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname*{lim}_{j}R^{\mathrm{dAff}}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K(\mathcal{X}).}

By construction, the right vertical map is an equivalence. We claim that, under the stated hypotheses, the bottom horizontal map is an equivalence. Indeed,

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{dAff}}K(\mathcal{X})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{lim}_{\alpha}K(X_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{lim}_{\alpha}\operatorname*{lim}_{j}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K(X_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{lim}_{j}\operatorname*{lim}_{\alpha}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K(X_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{lim}_{j}R^{\mathrm{dAff}}\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{<j}K(\mathcal{X}),

where we have used the hypothesis on the
X_{\alpha}
’s for the second equivalence.

∎

Theorem 9.2.6
.

Fix
G
a reductive group over a characteristic zero field
k
. Let
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow BG
be representable morphism where
\mathcal{X}
is derived algebraic stack, such that the morphism
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow BG
is finite type. Assume further that
\mathcal{X}
is ANS. Then we have canonical equivalences:

(9.2.7)

K(\mathcal{X})^{\wedge}_{I_{G}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K(\mathcal{X})^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{mot}}.

Proof.

After Lemma
9.2.5
, it is enough to prove that such a
\mathcal{X}
can be written as a Nisnevich colimit of derived algebraic spaces whose motivic filtration is complete. Indeed, by Theorem
B.0.1
we may assume that
\mathcal{X}
admits a Nisnevich surjection from a derived algebraic space whose classical locus is finite type over
k
. Hence, the result follows from Theorem
C.0.1
.(3).
∎

Appendix A
Geometric version of HKR isomorphism (after Ben-Zvi–Nadler)

The goal of this appendix is to prove Theorem
A.3.4
(originally due to Ben-Zvi and Nadler), as well as fill in some technical details that were left out from Section
7
.

A.1.
Completions of vector bundles

The following result is Proposition
7.1.26
in the main text, whose proof we now supply.

Proposition A.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a convergent derived prestack over
\mathbb Q
and let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})_{\geqslant 0}
. Assume that for any
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
the
\pi_{0}(R)
-module
\pi_{0}(f^{*}(\mathcal{E}))
is finitely generated. Then the natural map

{\operatorname*{colim}_{n}}^{\mathrm{conv}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}),

where
\operatorname*{colim}^{\mathrm{conv}}
denotes the colimit in convergent prestacks, is an equivalence.

Remark A.1.2
.

In formulation above we implicitly used the following fact: if
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is an affine map of prestacks and
\mathcal{X}
is convergent, then
\mathcal{Y}
is convergent. In particular,
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
are convergent, if
\mathcal{X}
is.

Proof.

By Remark
3.1.6
it is enough to show that the map induces an equivalence on
R
-points for all
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an},[0,\infty)}_{\mathbb Q}
. Fixing
R
, this is enough to do check the equivalence on fibres over
\mathcal{X}(R)
. Fixing a point
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
and looking at the description of fibers of
({\operatorname*{colim}}_{n}^{\mathrm{conv}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{E}))(R)
and
(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))(R)
over it, we reduce to the affine case
\mathcal{X}=\operatorname{Spec}R
with
\mathcal{E}
given by the connective module
M:=f^{*}\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{D}(R)_{\geqslant 0}
.

By our assumption on
\mathcal{E}
there is a map
R^{\oplus m}\rightarrow M
inducing a surjection on
\pi_{0}
. It induces a closed embedding

\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\rightarrow\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(R^{\oplus m})\simeq\operatorname{Spec}R\times\mathbb A^{m}.

Moreover, if we take preimage of 0 under the resulting projection
\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\rightarrow\mathbb A^{m}
, the classical locus

(\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\times_{\mathbb A^{n}}\{0\})^{\mathrm{cl}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)^{\mathrm{cl}}
is exactly identified with the zero section
\operatorname{Spec}\pi_{0}(R)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)^{\mathrm{cl}}
. Thus, by Example
7.1.5
,
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)
agrees with derived completion of
\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)
along
\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\times_{\mathbb A^{n}}\{0\}
. By Remark
7.1.3
and Example
7.1.8
we get an equivalence

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\simeq\mathbf{V}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\times_{\mathbb A^{m}}(\mathbb A^{m})^{\wedge}_{0}\simeq\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\operatorname{Spec}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})}\Sym_{R}(M)).

We will show that for each
s\geqslant 0
the pro-systems

\{\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})}\Sym_{R}(M))\}_{n\in\mathbb N}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\{\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M))\}_{n\in\mathbb N}

are equivalent; since we are only interested in values on the truncated algebras this will give the claim.

The map from the left to the right is induced by scalar multiplication, and to construct the inverse it will be enough to show that the map

\tau_{\leqslant s}\Sym_{R}(M)\rightarrow\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})}\Sym_{R}(M))

naturally factors through
\tau_{\leqslant s}\Sym^{\leqslant N}_{R}(M)
for
N\gg 0
. Note that by (
7.1.20
), given an
\mathbb N
-graded animated
R
-algebra
B^{\star}
such that
B^{m}\simeq 0
for
m>N
, there is a natural equivalence of spaces of graded algebra maps

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{gr}}(\Sym^{\star}_{R}(V),B^{\star})\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{gr}}(\Sym^{\leqslant N}_{R}(V),B^{\star});

in other words, any graded
R
-algebra map
\Sym^{\star}_{R}(V)\rightarrow B
canonically factors through
\Sym^{\leqslant N}_{R}(V)
. Since the map above is naturally a graded map it remains to show that the
N
-th graded component of

\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym^{\star}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathbb Q^{\oplus m})}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M))\simeq\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym^{\star}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M))

is 0 for
N\gg n
.

Considering the cofiber of
R^{\oplus m}\rightarrow M
we get a fiber sequence

R^{\oplus m}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M^{\prime}[1]

for some connective
R
-module
M^{\prime}
. This endows
M
with increasing 2-step filtration
F_{\bullet}(M)
(
F_{i}(M)=0
for
i<0
,
F_{0}(M):=R^{\oplus m}
and
F_{i}(M)=M
for
i>0
), which induces an increasing filtration on
\Sym_{R}(M)
as
\Sym_{R}(R^{\oplus m})
-module. By
[
Lur18
, Construction 25.2.5.4]
its associated graded is given by the tensor product

\Sym_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{R}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M^{\prime}[1])

with the filtration degree given by the degree on
\Sym^{\star}_{A}(M^{\prime}[1])
.
By
[
Lur18
, Proposition 25.2.4.1]
,
\Sym^{i}_{A}(M^{\prime}[1])
is
i
-connective, thus the filtration above is exhaustive. As a result, after tensoring with
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})
over
\Sym^{\star}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})
we get an exhaustive filtration on
\Sym^{\leqslant k}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym^{\star}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M)
as graded
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})
-algebra with the associated graded
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{R}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M^{\prime}[1])
(and with the grading given by total grading). Since
\Sym^{i}_{R}(M^{\prime}[1])
is
i
-connective, we get that for a given
s\geqslant 0
the
N
-th graded component of
\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{R}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M^{\prime}[1]))
is 0 for
N\gg 0
(more precisely
N>n-s
). Using long exact sequences of homotopy groups to pass back from the associated graded to the original object (or, equivalently, using the corresponding spectral sequence) we get that the same is true for the truncation

\tau_{\leqslant s}(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})\otimes_{\Sym^{\star}_{R}(R^{\oplus m})}\Sym^{\star}_{R}(M)).

∎

Remark A.1.3
.

From the proof it also follows that the natural map

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathbf{V}^{\curlywedge}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})

to the thin formal completion along the zero section is an isomorphism. Indeed, it is enough to check this after pulling back to every
\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, where it reduces to the fact that the map

(\mathbb A^{n})_{0}^{\wedge}\rightarrow(\mathbb A^{n})_{0}^{\curlywedge}

is an isomorphism (e.g. by Lemma
7.1.15
).

The next results are meant to assuage the reader of the worry that colimits in convergent derived prestacks could be destructive. The embedding
\mathrm{dPStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}\rightarrow\mathrm{dPStk}
has a left adjoint which we denote
\mathcal{Y}\mapsto\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}}
. Via the identifications
\mathrm{dPStk}^{\mathrm{conv}}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{[0,\infty)},\mathrm{Spc})
,
\mathrm{dPStk}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{cn}},\mathrm{Spc})
this functor is calculated by restricting
\mathcal{Y}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spc}
to
\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{[0,\infty)}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}{}
.

As noted in
[
GR17a
, Chapter 3, 1.2.5]
it is not true that the pull-back functor

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}})

induced by the unit map
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}}
is an equivalence. Nevertheless, we note that this is true for the full subcategories spanned by bounded below objects.

Lemma A.1.4
.

For any prestack
\mathcal{Y}
the natural map
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}}
induces an equivalence

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}})_{>-\infty}\simeq D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{>-\infty}.

Furthermore, it induces an equivalence

\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}})\simeq\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Y}).

Proof.

Following
[
GR17a
, Chapter 2, Section 1.3]
let us denote by
\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y})\in\mathrm{dPStk}
the left Kan extension of the restriction
\mathcal{Y}_{|\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{[0,n]}}
(to
n
-truncated algebras) along the embedding
\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{[0,n]}\subset\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an}}
. For any prestack
\mathcal{Y}
we claim that the natural map
\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y})
induces an equivalence

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{>-\infty}\simeq D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y}))_{>-\infty}.

Note that
D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{>-\infty}=\operatorname*{colim}_{n}D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{\geqslant-n}
and
D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{\geqslant-n}\simeq D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{\geqslant 0}
via the shift
[n]
, so it is enough to show that

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{\geqslant 0}\simeq D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y}))_{\geqslant 0}.

Since both sides send colimits to limits in the
\mathcal{Y}
-variable, it is enough to check this for
\mathcal{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}R
. In this case
\tau^{\leqslant n}(\operatorname{Spec}R)
is represented by
\operatorname{Spec}\tau_{\leqslant n}R
and we need to prove that the functor

D(R)_{\geqslant 0}\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{\geqslant 0}

sending
M
to
\{M\otimes_{R}\tau_{\leqslant n}(R)\}_{n}
is an equivalence. Note that
D(A)
is left
t
-complete for any
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, and so
D(A)_{\geqslant 0}\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{m\geqslant 0}D(A)_{[0,m]}
(with map induced by truncations
\{\tau_{\leqslant m}\}
). We then have

\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{\geqslant 0}\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n,m\geqslant 0}D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{[0,m]}\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{[0,n]}.

Moreover, the corresponding functors
D(R)_{\geqslant 0}\rightarrow D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{[0,n]}
are given by the truncations

M\mapsto\tau_{\leqslant n}(M\otimes_{R}\tau_{\leqslant n}R)\simeq\tau_{\leqslant n}M

where the right equivalence holds since the fiber of the map
R\rightarrow\tau_{\leqslant n}R
is
(n+1)
-connective. This way (under the equivalence
D(R)_{[0,n]}\simeq D(\tau_{\leqslant n}R)_{[0,n]}
induced by
\tau_{\leqslant n}
) the functor
D(R)_{\geqslant 0}\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(\tau_{\leqslant n}(R))_{[0,n]}
can be identified with the left
t
-completion functor

D(R)_{\geqslant 0}\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(R)_{[0,n]}.

It is an equivalence by left
t
-completeness of
D(R)
. Finally, for any
n\geqslant 0
we have
\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y})\simeq\tau^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}})
(since the restrictions of
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}}
to
\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{<\infty}
canonically agree) and so

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}})_{>-\infty}\simeq D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{Y})_{>-\infty}.

The equivalence for
\mathcal{O}(-)
is proved similarly, by using that for
\mathcal{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}R
one has

R\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}\tau_{\leqslant n}R.

∎

Corollary A.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a prestack over
\mathbb Q
. Let
\mathcal{E}\in D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})_{\geqslant 0}
and assume that for any
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
the
\pi_{0}(R)
-module
\pi_{0}(f^{*}(\mathcal{E}))
is finitely generated. Then the map
\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}(\mathcal{E})
induces natural equivalences

\mathcal{O}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\mathcal{O}(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\Sym^{i}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathcal{E}))).

Proof.

Functor
\mathcal{Y}\mapsto\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{conv}}
commutes with colimits and so

(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))^{\mathrm{conv}}\simeq{\operatorname*{colim}_{n}}^{\mathrm{conv}}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})

By Lemma
A.1.4
we then get

\mathcal{O}({\operatorname*{colim}_{n}}^{\mathrm{conv}}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\mathcal{O}(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})),

and the first isomorphism follows from Proposition
A.1.1
.
Since
\mathcal{Y}\mapsto\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Y})\in D(\mathbb Q)
sends colimits to limits we also get

\mathcal{O}(\operatorname*{colim}_{n}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant n}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n}\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\leqslant n}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n}(\oplus_{i=0}^{n}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\Sym^{i}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathcal{E})))\simeq\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\Sym^{i}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathcal{E}))).

∎

Remark A.1.6
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived algebraic stack over an affine derived scheme
S
. Then for any
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
the
\pi_{0}(R)
-module
\pi_{0}(f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
is finitely generated. Indeed, by
[
Stacks
, Tag 03C4]
we can check finite generatedness flat locally. Let
p\colon U\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a smooth cover by a 0-Artin stack
U
and let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

be the fiber product; since
\mathcal{X}
is algebraic
Z
is a derived algebraic space. We have a fiber sequence

g^{*}\mathbb L_{U/\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow g^{*}p^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]\rightarrow g^{*}\mathbb L_{U/S}[1]

where
\mathbb L_{U/S}
is connective (since
U
is 0-Artin) and
\mathbb L_{U/\mathcal{X}}
is a finite locally free sheaf on
U
(by smoothness of
p
). Thus we get a surjection

\pi_{0}(g^{*}\mathbb L_{U/\mathcal{X}})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(g^{*}p^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\simeq\pi_{0}(q^{*}f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])

where
\pi_{0}(g^{*}\mathbb L_{U/\mathcal{X}})
is locally finite free, which shows that
\pi_{0}(q^{*}f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
is locally finite.

Lemma A.1.7

(Nakayama lemma)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a prestack. Let
\mathcal{E}\in D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})_{\geqslant 0}
and assume that for any
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
the
\pi_{0}(R)
-module
\pi_{0}(f^{*}(\mathcal{E}))
is finitely generated. Let
z_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
be the zero section map. Then the pull-back functor

z_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\colon D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))_{<\infty}\rightarrow D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})_{<\infty}

is conservative.

Proof.

Assume
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))_{<\infty}
is such that
z_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq 0
but
\mathcal{F}\not\simeq 0
. Then there exists a map
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
such that
f^{*}\mathcal{F}\not\simeq 0
. Note that
f
factors
21
21

21

Indeed,
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(f^{*}\mathcal{E})
is identified with the fiber product
\operatorname{Spec}R\times_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
where the map
\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is given by composition of
f
with the projection
p_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
and where
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is the same projection.
through the natural map
\tilde{f}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(f^{*}\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
and thus
\tilde{f}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is also non-zero. Since we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(f^{*}\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{\operatorname{Spec}R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathcal{X}}\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{\mathcal{X}}}

it will be enough to show the statement of the lemma for
\mathcal{X}=\operatorname{Spec}R
and a connective
R
-module
M:=f^{*}\mathcal{E}
.

By Proposition
A.1.1
and Lemma
A.1.4
we have an equivalence

D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M))_{>-\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D_{\mathrm{qc}}({\mathbf{V}}^{\leqslant n}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M))_{>-\infty}\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{n\geqslant 0}D(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M))_{>-\infty}

and so given a non-zero
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M))_{>-\infty}
the pull-back
z_{n}^{*}\mathcal{F}\in D(\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M))_{>-\infty}
is also non-zero for some
n\geqslant 0
(here
z_{n}\colon{\mathbf{V}}^{\leqslant n}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\operatorname{Spec}R}(M)
is the natural map). To arrive at a contradiction we need to show that
z_{0}^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq z_{\operatorname{Spec}R}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is also non-zero.

Note that
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M)
as a module over itself has a finite filtration by
\Sym^{[n-i,n]}_{R}(M)\subset\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M)
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant n
, such that
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M)
-action on the associated graded factors through the projection to the 0-th graded component
\Sym^{\leqslant n}_{R}(M)\rightarrow R
. This gives a finite filtration on
z_{n}^{*}\mathcal{F}
with associated graded terms of the form
z_{\operatorname{Spec}R}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}\Sym^{i}_{R}(M)
, from which we see that if
z_{\operatorname{Spec}R}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is zero then so should be
z_{n}^{*}\mathcal{F}
.

∎

A.2.
Descent for cotangent complex

Below we will also need a descent statement for the exterior powers of the cotangent complex.

Proposition A.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived geometric stack over an affine derived scheme
S
. Then for any
n
the natural map

(A.2.2)

\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}x_{*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])

with
T\in\mathrm{dAff}_{S}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Note that the functor
(x\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X})\mapsto x_{*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])
from
\mathrm{dAff}_{/\mathcal{X}}
to
D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})
satisfies étale and, consequently, smooth descent. Indeed, having an étale cover
f\colon T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
we have
\mathbb L_{T^{\prime}/T}\simeq 0
, so
f^{*}\mathbb L_{T^{\prime}/S}\simeq\mathbb L_{T/S}
and, more generally,
f^{*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])\simeq\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}}(\mathbb L_{T^{\prime}/S}[1])
. Then for the corresponding Čech cover
f_{\bullet}\colon T_{\bullet}^{\prime}\rightarrow T
the map

\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}f_{\bullet*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}}(\mathbb L_{T^{\prime}/S}[1])\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}f_{\bullet*}f^{*}_{\bullet}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])

is an equivalence by flat descent for quasi-coherent sheaves.

It is easy to see that if
\mathcal{X}
is 0-Artin the map in (
A.2.2
) is an equivalence: namely, if
\mathcal{X}=\sqcup_{i}U_{i}
with
s_{i}\colon U_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
being the embedding of a connected component, we have

\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\simeq\prod_{i}(s_{i})_{*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}}(\mathbb L_{U_{i}/S}[1]).

For general
\mathcal{X}
, the right hand side of (
A.2.2
) is the value on the final object of the right Kan extension along
\mathrm{dAff}_{/\mathcal{X}}\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{dAff}_{/\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Spc})
of the functor
(x\colon T\rightarrow\mathcal{X})\mapsto x_{*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])
. By smooth descent we can replace the final object by the geometric realization of the Čech nerve
q_{\bullet}\colon U_{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of some smooth cover
U_{0}:=U\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{X}
by a 0-Artin stack
U
. It then remains to show that the map

\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\!(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}q_{\bullet*}\Sym^{n}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\bullet}}}(\mathbb L_{U_{\bullet}/S}[1])

is an equivalence. This can be done as in
[
KP22
, Proposition 1.1.5]
replacing
\Lambda^{n}
with
\Sym^{n}
in the proof.
∎

A.3.
Formal loops

Let
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathrm{an}}
be a base connective ring.
For a (not necessarily connective) derived commutative
R
-algebra
C\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb Q}
let
\operatorname{Spec}C\in\mathrm{dPStk}_{R}
denote the functor corepresented by
C
:

(\operatorname{Spec}C)(A):=\Maps_{\mathrm{CAlg}{}_{R}^{\mathrm{an}}}(C,A)

for
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an}}_{R}
. For any prestack
\mathcal{X}\in\mathrm{dPStk}_{R}
one has a natural “affinization map”

a_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{X}),

and any map to a derived affine scheme from
\mathcal{X}
factors through
s_{\mathcal{X}}
.
If
\mathcal{X}=\underline{K}
is the constant
R
-prestack given by a homotopy type
K\in\mathrm{Spc}
, then

\mathcal{O}(\underline{K})\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{K}\mathcal{O}(\operatorname{Spec}R)\simeq\operatorname*{lim}_{K}k\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K,R),

thus we get a map

a_{\underline{K}}\colon\underline{K}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K,R)).

In the case
K\simeq S^{1}
and
\mathbb Q\subset R
one can further identify
22
22

22

The reason is that the splitting
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},R)\simeq R\oplus R[-1]
as
R
-modules induces an equivalence
\Sym_{R}R[-1]\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},R)
, if
R
is a
\mathbb Q
-algebra. Then one computes
\Maps_{\mathrm{CAlg}{}_{R}^{\mathrm{an}}}(\Sym_{R}R[-1],A)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}(A[1])\simeq(B\mathbb G_{a})(A)
.
(
[
BZN12
, Lemma 3.13]
)

\operatorname{Spec}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},R))\simeq B\mathbb G_{a,R}

Construction A.3.1
.

Let
S:=\operatorname{Spec}R
and assume that
R
is a
\mathbb Q
-algebra. For an affine derived
S
-scheme
T=\operatorname{Spec}A
the affinization map

\underline{S^{1}}\rightarrow B\mathbb G_{a,R}

induces an equivalence

\mathcal{L}T:={\underline{\Maps}}_{/S}(\underline{S^{1}},T)\simeq\underline{\Maps}_{/S}(B\mathbb G_{a,R},T).

Following
23
23

23

More precisely, identifying
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},R)\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}
with the split square zero extension
R\oplus R[-1]
, they use obstruction theory in terms of cotangent complex.

[
BZN12
, Proposition 4.4]
we can further identify

\underline{\Maps}_{/S}(B\mathbb G_{a},T)\simeq\mathbf{V}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1]):=\operatorname{Spec}(\Sym_{A}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])).

This gives a derived version of Hochshild-Kostant-Rosenberg isomorphism
24
24

24

Both sides are derived affine schemes and after passing to global functions one gets an isomorphism
HH(A)\simeq\Sym_{A}^{\star}(\mathbb L_{A}[1])\simeq\oplus_{i}(\Lambda^{i}_{A}\mathbb L_{A})[i].
:

(A.3.2)

\mathbf{V}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{L}_{S}T.

Let now
\mathcal{X}
be a geometric stack over
S
. Let
x\colon T=\operatorname{Spec}A\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a map from an affine derived scheme. For each
n
and
m
we consider a composition

\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])\rightarrow\mathbf{V}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])\simeq\mathcal{L}_{S}T\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{p}\mathcal{X},

where
p\colon\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}=X\times_{X\times_{S}X}X\rightarrow X
.
Since
\mathcal{X}
is geometric, its diagonal is affine, and thus so is
p
, as well as the whole composition above. The direct image of the structure sheaf under it is identified with
(x)_{*}\Sym^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}\!(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])
. Taking a limit over all
x
we get a natural map of algebras in
D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}x_{*}\Sym^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1]),

where
T
in the limit runs over all derived affine schemes over
\mathcal{X}
. Recall that the natural map

\Sym^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\operatorname*{lim}_{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}(x)_{*}\Sym^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])

is an equivalence by Proposition
A.2.1
.

This way, for each
m
we get a natural map

p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\Sym^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]),

which, by passing to
\operatorname{Spec}_{\mathcal{X}}(-)
and taking colimit over
m
, gives a map

{\operatorname*{colim}_{m}}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X};

note that here we again used that
\mathcal{X}
is geometric to identify
\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}\simeq\operatorname{Spec}_{\mathcal{X}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
.

Since
\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
is convergent (Remark
7.2.3
) this map factors through the similar colimit in convergent prestacks, giving a map

(A.3.3)

\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\simeq{\operatorname*{colim}_{m}}^{\mathrm{conv}}\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}

with the first identification provided by Proposition
A.1.1
and Remark
A.1.6
.

The pre-composition of
\exp_{\mathcal{X}}
with the zero section
z\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
is naturally identified with the embedding of constant loops
c_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
. This gives a factorization
25
25

25

More precisely, completion of
\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])
at the zero section does nothing, so completing both
\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])
and
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}
along
\mathcal{X}

gives a natural map
\mathbf{V}^{\leqslant m}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{X}
for any
m
.
of (
A.3.3
) through the formal completion
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}
, producing a map

\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}.

The goal of this subsection is to prove the following:

Theorem A.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived geometric stack over a derived affine
\mathbb Q
-scheme
S
. Then the map

\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Prestacks
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}
are completions of derived algebraic stacks
\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
and so admit corepresentable deformation theory in the sense of
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Definition 7.1.2]
(see also
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Proposition 7.4.2]
and Remark
A.3.5
).

Following Ben-Zvi–Nadler (
[
BZN12
, Section 6.3.2]
), the key computation is that the relative cotangent complex

\mathbb L_{\exp_{\mathcal{X}}}:=\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])/\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}}

vanishes. Let us explain how this vanishing implies the statement of the theorem. By Remark
7.1.4
(1)
both sides are convergent stacks, so it is enough to show that
\exp_{\mathcal{X}}
induces an equivalence on
R
-points for bounded
R
.

Recall the description of the formal completion in Remark
7.1.4
(3)
. For a nilpotent ideal
I\subset\pi_{0}(R)
denote
F_{I,R}\subset\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])(R)
the subspace given by those
R
-points which send
\operatorname{Spec}(\pi_{0}(R)/I)
to
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
. Similarly, let
G_{I,R}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}(R)
be the subspace given by
R
-points which send
\operatorname{Spec}(\pi_{0}(R)/I)
to
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}
. By the Definition
7.1.1
of (absolute) formal completion the natural maps of spaces

\left(\operatorname*{colim}_{I\subset\pi_{0}(R)}F_{I,R}\right)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])(R),\qquad\left(\operatorname*{colim}_{I\subset\pi_{0}(R)}G_{I,R}\right)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}(R)

are equivalences. Thus it would be enough to show that the natural map

F_{I,R}\rightarrow G_{I,R}

is an equivalence for all nilpotent ideals
I\subset\pi_{0}(R)
and bounded rings
R
. By the argument in
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Proposition 5.5.3]
, any such
R
is obtained
from
\pi_{0}(R)/I
via a
finite
amount of square-zero extensions:

R=R_{n}\rightarrow R_{n-1}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow R_{0}=\pi_{0}(R)/I.

We will argue by induction on
i
in
R_{i}
. For
i=0
we have a diagram of isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{I,R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{I,R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}(R_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

Then, assuming the map
F_{I,R_{i}}\rightarrow G_{I,R_{i}}
is an equivalence we need to show that so is
F_{I,R_{i+1}}\rightarrow G_{I,R_{i+1}}
. By deformation theory
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Lemma 10.2.6]
, the fiber of the natural map

F_{I,R_{i+1}}\rightarrow F_{I,R_{i}}

over an
R_{i}
-point
s\colon\operatorname{Spec}R_{i}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
(sending
\operatorname{Spec}(\pi_{0}(R)/I)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}R_{i}
to
\mathcal{X}
) is given by the space of nil-homotopies of the composition

s^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])}\rightarrow\mathbb L_{\operatorname{Spec}R_{i}/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{I}_{i}[1]

where the second map is the one defining the square-zero extension
R_{i+1}\rightarrow R_{i}
(see
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Section 5.1]
,
\mathcal{I}_{i}
is the “ideal” of the square-zero extension). Similarly, the fibers of
G_{I,R_{i+1}}\rightarrow G_{I,R_{i}}
are described in terms of the cotangent complex of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}
.

Now, given a point
s\in F_{I,R_{i}}\subset\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])(R_{i})
we have a fiber sequence

(\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\circ s)^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow s^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])}\rightarrow s^{*}\mathbb L_{\exp_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Since the third term vanishes, by the above we get that
\exp_{\mathcal{X}}
induces an isomorphism between the fiber of
F_{I,R_{i+1}}
over
s\in F_{I,R_{i}}
and the fiber of
G_{I,R_{i+1}}
over
\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\circ s\in G_{I,R_{i}}
correspondingly. Since this is true for any
s\in F_{I,R_{i}}
, we get that

\exp_{\mathcal{X}}\colon F_{I,R_{i+1}}\rightarrow G_{I,R_{i+1}}

is an equivalence.

It remains to show that
\mathbb L_{\exp_{\mathcal{X}}}\simeq 0
.
By Lemma
A.1.7
it is enough to show this after restricting to the zero section
z_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])
, which is equivalent to

\exp_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\colon c^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow z^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])/\mathcal{X}}

being a quasi-isomorphism (here we consider relative cotangent complexes with respect to projections
q_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
and
p_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
).

Let
x\colon T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a map where
T
is a derived affine scheme and let
c_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
and
c_{T}\colon T\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{S}T
denote the embeddings of constant loops. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

which induces a natural map

\mathcal{L}(x)^{*}\colon c^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow x_{*}c_{T}^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{L}_{S}T/T}.

By the argument of
[
BZN12
, Lemma 6.7]

c^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}}
is naturally identified with
\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]
, further identifying the map above with the pull-back map

\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]\rightarrow x_{*}\mathbb L_{T/S}[1].

Similarly, we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}(x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

where
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}(x)
is induced by the map
\mathbb L_{T/S}[1]\rightarrow x^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]
. It induces the corresponding map

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}(x)^{*}\colon z^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}}[1])/\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow x_{*}z_{T}^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T}[1])/T},

which via Remark
A.3.6
can also be naturally identified with the pull-back map

\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]\rightarrow x_{*}\mathbb L_{T/S}[1].

Finally, we also have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exp_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exp_{T}}

lying under and above
\mathcal{X}\leftarrow T
. Recall that essentially by construction,
\exp_{T}
is an equivalence. Together with the maps above, and taking a limit over all
x\colon T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
we get a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}{\operatorname*{lim}}(x_{*}c_{T}^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{L}_{S}T/T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1])/\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}{\operatorname*{lim}}(x_{*}z_{T}^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{T}(\mathbb L_{T/S}[1])/T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exp_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname*{lim}\mathcal{L}(x)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname*{lim}\exp_{T}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname*{lim}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(x)^{*}}

where the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism since
\exp_{T}
is. Moreover, by the above discussion both horizontal arrows are identified with the natural map

\mathbb L_{\mathcal{X}/S}[1]\rightarrow\underset{x:T\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}{\operatorname*{lim}}x_{*}\mathbb L_{T/S}[1],

which is an equivalence by Proposition
A.2.1
. Thus
\exp_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
is also an equivalence.
∎

Remark A.3.5
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a map of convergent derived prestacks and assume that
\mathcal{Y}
admits corepresentable deformation theory in the sense of
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Definition 7.1.2]
. Then the derived completion
\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}
also admits corepresentable deformation theory. Indeed,
\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}
is also convergent, and the conditions on admitting cotangent complex (
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Definitions 4.1.4]
) and being infinitesimally cohesive (
[
GR17b
, Chapter 1, Definition 6.1.2]
) reduce to the ones for
\mathcal{Y}
. Moreover, the relative cotangent complex
\mathbb L_{\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}/\mathcal{Y}}
for the natural map
\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is 0; in particular,

f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}}\simeq f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{Y}}

(since the cofiber of this map is given by
f^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathcal{Y}^{\wedge}_{f}/\mathcal{Y}}
).

Remark A.3.6
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived prestack admitting a cotangent complex, let
\mathcal{E}\in D_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{X})_{\geqslant 0}
and consider the corresponding vector bundle
\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
, with the zero section and projection maps
z\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
and
p\colon\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
. We claim that there is a natural identification

z^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})/\mathcal{X}}\simeq\mathcal{E}.

Indeed, for any map
s\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
and any
M\in D(R)_{\geqslant 0}
the space of maps
\operatorname{Spec}(R\oplus M)\rightarrow\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
extending
s
is identified with the space of
R
-algebra maps
\Sym_{R}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\rightarrow R\oplus M
which commute with the natural projections to
R
on both sides. By the universal property of
\Sym_{R}
the latter is naturally identified with
\Hom_{R}(s^{*}\mathcal{E},M)
, and so
z^{*}\mathbb L_{\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}
and
\mathcal{E}
corepresent the same functor. By Remark
A.3.5
we also get a formula for the relative cotangent complex of the formal completion

z^{*}\mathbb L_{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})/\mathcal{X}}\simeq\mathcal{E}.

Lemma A.3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived algebraic stack over an affine
\mathbb Q
-scheme
S
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\!\curlywedge}_{S}\mathcal{X}
be the absolute and thin formal completions of the relative loop stack
\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
along constant loops. Then the natural map

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\!\curlywedge}_{S}\mathcal{X}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
7.1.15
. Indeed, by
[
Stacks
, tag 04XS]
the map of classical stacks
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow(\mathcal{X}\times_{S}\mathcal{X})^{\mathrm{cl}}
is of locally of finite type, and so by
[
Stacks
, tag 0CMG]
the second diagonal map
c_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X})^{\mathrm{cl}}
is locally of finite presentation.
∎

The following lemma shows that the completion at constant loops passes along representable maps.

Lemma A.3.8

(
[
Che20
, Proposition 2.1.20]
)
.

Let
S
be a derived affine scheme. Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a map of derived algebraic
S
-stacks representable in derived algebraic spaces. Then the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{S}\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{Y}}

is a fiber square.

Proof.

To simplify notation we let
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}:=\mathcal{L}_{S}\mathcal{X}
, and similary for the completion.
By Remark
7.1.3
fiber product
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Y}
is identified with the formal completion of
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}
along the map
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}
. Thus by Remark
7.1.4
(3)
it will be enough to show that the natural map

\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{Y}

is an equivalence when restricted to classical rings. For this we need to check that the map of classical stacks

(A.3.9)

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow\mathcal{I}({\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}})\times_{\mathcal{I}({\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}})}\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}

is an isomorphism. Here, for a classical
S^{\mathrm{cl}}
-stack
\mathcal{Z}
,
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{Z}:=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{Z}\times_{S^{\mathrm{cl}}}\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{Z}
denotes the relative inertia stack of
\mathcal{X}
.
The projection
\mathcal{I}({\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}
induces a projection
\mathcal{I}({\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}})\times_{\mathcal{I}({\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}})}\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}
whose composition with (
A.3.9
) is the identity. It is enough to show that (
A.3.9
) induces an equivalence on fibers over all maps
x\colon\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}
from (classical) affine schemes. The map between fibers is identified with the map of algebraic spaces

\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(x)\times_{\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(f^{\mathrm{cl}}(x)}\operatorname{Spec}R.

where
\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(x):=\operatorname{Spec}R\times_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}}\operatorname{Spec}R
(with maps given by
x
) is the automorphism group (algebraic space) of
x
, the map
\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(f^{\mathrm{cl}}(x))
is given by the unit element, and the map to the fiber product is induced by the analogous map
\operatorname{Spec}R\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(x)
and the identity on
\operatorname{Spec}R
.
Note that
f^{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}
is represented by classical algebraic spaces, and so by
[
Stacks
, tag 04YY]
the kernel of the map
\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(x)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}}(f^{\mathrm{cl}}(x))}
is trivial. In particular, the preimage of the identity map is just the identity map itself, so the map above is an isomorphism.
∎

Appendix B
Derived Deshmukh’s theorem

The goal of this appendix is to recall (and extend to the derived setting) the theorem of Deshmukh.

Theorem B.0.1

(
[
Des23
, Theorem 1.2(1)]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a quasi-compact quasi-separated derived algebraic stack with separated diagonal. Then there is a smooth-Nisnevich surjection
Y\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, with
Y
being a derived affine scheme.

In fact the epimorphism in the construction will be a
smooth-Nisnevich covering
in the following sense:

Definition B.0.2

(
[
Des23
, Definition 1.1]
)
.

We say that a representable map of derived algebraic stacks
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is a
smooth-Nisnevich covering
, if

•

it is smooth;

•

the map

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{X}(K))\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(K))

is surjective for any field
K
.

It is clear that smooth-Nisnevich coverings are closed under composition.

We have the following lemma:

Lemma B.0.3
.

Smooth-Nisnevich coverings of derived algebraic stacks are Nisnevich surjections.

Proof.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a smooth-Nisnevich covering. We need to show that for an
R
-point
y\in\mathcal{Y}(R)
for
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an}}
there exists a Nisnevich cover
R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
and a point
x\in\mathcal{X}(R^{\prime})
lifting
y
. Since both
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are convergent it is enough to assume that
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an},[0,\infty)}
.

First, let us assume
R\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{cl}}
. Note that
[
Des23
, Corollary 2.7]
shows that the map

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{O})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{O})

is a surjection on connected components if
\mathcal{O}
is a Henselian ring. Since
f
is smooth it is, in particular, locally finitely presented. Setting
\mathcal{O}
to be the henselizations of points of
\operatorname{Spec}R
, it follows that for any point
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}(R)
there exists a Nisnevich covering
R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
and a point
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(R^{\prime})
lifting
y
.

Now, given
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{\mathrm{an},[0,\infty)}
with
\pi_{0}(A)\simeq R
and an
A
-point
y\in\mathcal{Y}(A)
we can lift the corresponding point
\pi_{0}(y)\in\mathcal{Y}(R)
to a point
x\in\mathcal{X}(R^{\prime})
. Since
R\rightarrow R^{\prime}
is étale we can uniquely extend it to an étale map
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
, which is then also a Nisnevich cover. Finally, since
f
is smooth, the deformation theory for lifting a map along
f
is unobstructed
26
26

26

Here we implicitly use that
A^{\prime}
(being flat over
A
) also belongs to
\mathrm{CAlg}{}^{[0,\infty)}
, and that it can be obtained from
R^{\prime}\simeq\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})
by a finite set of square-zero extensions, as in
[
GR17b
, Proposition 5.5.3]
.
, and so
x\in\mathcal{X}(R^{\prime})
extends to an
A^{\prime}
-point
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}(A^{\prime})
lifting
y\in\mathcal{Y}(A)
.
∎

To construct the required smooth-Nisnevich covering we will need the following:

Construction B.0.4
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a representable morphism of derived algebraic stacks whose diagonal is separated.

Consider the
n
-fold fiber product
(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{n}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\ldots\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{X}
. The
S=\operatorname{Spec}R
-point of
(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{n}
is given by an
S
-point
S\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of
\mathcal{Y}
and
n
-sections
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
of the induced map
\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}S\rightarrow S
. One defines the
stack of
n
-section

\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
as the substack of
(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{n}
where the classical loci of points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\colon S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}S
do not intersect; since the diagonal of
f
is separated this is in fact a Zariski open substack. The symmetric group
\Sigma_{n}
acts naturally on
(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{n}
, as well as
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\subset(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{n}
. One defines the
stack of finite étale substacks of degree
n
as the quotient

\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}):=[\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})/\Sigma_{n}]

in étale topology. The natural projection
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is representable in derived algebraic spaces: indeed, it is clear for
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, so
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is at least represented in derived Deligne-Mumford stacks. But then the action of
\Sigma_{n}
on the fibers of the map
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})^{\mathrm{cl}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{cl}}
is free, which shows that the quotient by
\Sigma_{n}
is in fact represented in derived algebraic spaces. It is also clear that the map
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is smooth if
f
is.

If
\mathcal{X}
is a derived algebraic space, then so is
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
; consequently
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
is a derived Deligne-Mumford stack. We warn the reader that the action of
\Sigma_{n}
is free on
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
only relative to
\mathcal{Y}
, and not globally, so
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
is not necessarily an algebraic space.
27
27

27

As an example consider the map
f\colon[\mathbb A^{1}/\mathbb G_{m}]\rightarrow B\mathbb G_{m}
. Then
\mathrm{Sec}_{2}(f)
is identified with
[(\mathbb A^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta(\mathbb A^{1}))/\mathbb G_{m}]
. The
\Sigma_{2}
-action on the latter sends
(x,y)
to
(y,x)
, but note that if
x=-y
, then these points are identified via multiplication by
-1\in\mathbb G_{m}
, and so define the same point in the quotient.
If
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are qcqs then both
\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
and
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})
are also qcqs.

Proof of Theorem
B.0.1
:
We will construct a smooth-Nisnevich covering
Y\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
by a countable union of qcqs algebraic spaces, by Knutson-Lurie theorem (Theorem
4.3.11
) we then can refine this to a covering by countable union of derived affine schemes. Now let
p\colon U\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a smooth atlas where
U
is affine and consider the map
\sqcup_{n}\mathrm{Et}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
. Since
p
is smooth, it is an étale surjection, so any map
x\colon\operatorname{Spec}k\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
lifts to a map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}\ell}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}

from a finite extension
\ell/k
. But a map
\operatorname{Spec}\ell\rightarrow U
lifting
x
gives a section
\operatorname{Spec}k\rightarrow\mathrm{Et}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})
with
n:=\deg(\ell/k)
lying over
x
. This way we get that
\sqcup_{n}\mathrm{Et}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is a smooth-Nisnevich surjection. Unfortunately,
\mathrm{Et}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})
are not necessarily derived algebraic spaces, but could possibly be derived Deligne-Mumford stacks. However, this is easy to fix, following the argument in
[
LMB00
, Théorème 6.1]
for classical stacks; namely for each
n
consider the natural embedding
\Sigma_{n}\hookrightarrow GL_{n}
and define
Z_{n}:=[(\mathrm{Sec}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})\times GL_{n})/\Sigma_{n}]
where the action of
\Sigma_{n}
is diagonal. Each
Z_{n}
is a derived algebraic space (since the action of
\Sigma_{n}
is now free), and there is a natural map
Z_{n}\rightarrow\sqcup_{n}\mathrm{Et}_{n}(U/\mathcal{X})
which is a
GL_{n}
-torsor. Since
GL_{n}
-torsors over fields are trivial, the latter map is a smooth-Nisnevich covering. The resulting map

\sqcup_{n}Z_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}

is then the desired smooth-Nisnevich covering by a countable union of qcqs algebraic spaces.

Appendix C
Motivic filtrations on algebraic spaces

In this appendix, we record the following tiny extension of motivic filtrations on the
K
-theory of qcqs derived algebraic spaces. Most of these results are direct consequences of the Knutson-Lurie Theorem
4.3.11
. We briefly recall our conventions regarding filtrations which follows
[
EM23
]
. For
\mathcal{C}
a stable
\infty
-category,
\infty
-categories of
filtered objects
is given by
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}}:=\mathrm{Fun}((\mathbb{Z},\geqslant)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{C})
, where
(\mathbb{Z},\geqslant)
denotes the totally ordered set of the integers and
\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}
is the discrete category of the integers. If
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Spt}
, then we denote this by
\mathrm{FiltSpt}
. We write filtered objects of
\mathcal{C}
as
\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}M
, where
M
is an object of
\mathcal{C}
which implicitly means that there is a morphism
\mathrm{Fil}^{-\infty}M:=\operatorname*{colim}_{j\to\-\infty}\mathrm{Fil}^{j}M\to M
in
\mathcal{C}
. The filtration is said to be
exhaustive
when the latter morphism is an equivalence; more strongly we say that it is
\mathbb{N}
-indexed
when
\mathrm{Fil}^{j}M\to M
is an equivalence for all
j\leqslant 0
. The filtration is said to be
complete
if
\operatorname*{lim}_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{Fil}^{j}M=0
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is presentably symmetric monoida, then
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}}
admit canonical symmetric monoidal structures given by Day convolution. In particular, we have the
\infty
-category of
filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras

\mathrm{CAlg}{}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}})
. A filtered object with underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra object
M
which is compatibly a
\mathbb{E}{\infty}
-algebra in filtered objects is called a
multiplicative filtration
.

Theorem C.0.1

(Motivic filtrations on derived algebraic spaces)
.

There exists a finitary,
\mathbb{N}
-indexed, multiplicative Nisnevich sheaf of filtered spectra

(C.0.2)

\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K:(\mathrm{dAlgSpc}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{FilSpt}).

with the following properties:

(1)

for any
X\in\mathrm{dAlgSpc}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
, its graded pieces are given by

\mathrm{gr}^{j}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)\simeq R^{\mathrm{dAff}}\mathbb{Z}(j)^{\mathrm{mot}}(X)[2j]

We set for any
X\in\mathrm{dAlgSpc}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
,
H^{i}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X;\mathbb{Z}(j)):=H^{i}(R^{\mathrm{dAff}}\mathbb{Z}(j)^{\mathrm{mot}}(X))
, and obtain an Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}:=H^{i-j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X;\mathbb{Z}(-j))\Rightarrow K_{-i-j}(X).

(2)

If
X
admits a Nisnevich cover by derived affine schemes
\{X_{i}\rightarrow X\}
where the classical locus
X_{i}^{\mathrm{cl}}
has finite valuative dimension, then the filtration is complete and the Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence is convergent.

(3)

the Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence degenerates rationally and

K_{n}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong\bigoplus_{j\geqslant 0}H_{\mathrm{mot}}^{2j-n}(X;\mathbb{Z}(j))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
4.3.13
, the formation of algebraic
K
-theory on qcqs derived algebraic spaces is right Kan extended from derived affine schemes. Hence, the filtration (
C.0.2
) is defined by taking the right Kan extension of the motivic filtrations built in
[
Bou24
,
EM23
]
from derived affine schemes; in particular the underlying object of the filtration remains algebraic
K
-theory because the latter is right Kan extended from derived affine schemes. The claimed properties are then formal consequences of this definition and the equivalence (
4.3.12
).

The only thing to explain concerns the completeness claim of (2). If
X
is a derived algebraic space, we note that
\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)
is complete whenever
\operatorname*{lim}_{\star\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)\simeq 0
. By (
4.3.12
) we can write
\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)
as the totalization of
\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(Y^{\bullet})
where each
Y^{n}
has the property that
\operatorname*{lim}\mathrm{Fil}^{\star}_{\mathrm{mot}}K(Y^{n})\simeq 0
; see
[
Bou24
, Proposition 4.50]
for the latter completeness result. The claim then follows from the fact that limits commute.

∎
